		\paragraph{Isaiah 1:1} % *ISAIAH-1-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-1-1}

			The vision of Isaiah the son of Amoz, which he saw concerning Judah and Jerusalem in the days of Uzziah, Jotham, Ahaz, and Hezekiah, kings of Judah.

			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					% \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 1:2} % *ISAIAH-1-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-1-2}

			Hear, O heavens, and give ear, O earth:%
				\footnote{%
					NICOT on Isaiah 1--39, 85: ``In the opening words, the heavens and earth are called to listen in on what amounts to a divine monologue. God is meditating on the strange situation that has developed. The tone makes it clear that what follows will be not so much a legal presentation as a personal one. While the covenant is clearly in view here, it is in the background and remains there. Israel's offense is against common decency and common sense. Even animals know better.
					
					``The opening appeal is strongly reminiscent of Deut. 32: 1 and 30:19, where Moses called upon the heavens and the earth to witness the covenant of blessing or curse. It seems likely that Isaiah consciously reverts to the Deuteronomic language here. The covenant has been broken and its curses fall upon the people. Likewise, the use of the term \textit{Israel} in v. 3 probably points up Isaiah's consciousness of the covenant, as does the use of \textit{know} in the same verse.
					
					``Isaiah's references to the covenant are not nearly as explicit as those of Jeremiah. In fact, Isaiah does not use the word \hyperref[BERIT]{\hel{b*:riyt}}. `covenant.' Yet it cannot be denied that Isaiah knows of the covenant. It appears to be the ground of all his thinking, but not a source for legal appeal. Rather, it is a pattern for living, without which life cannot be sustained.
					
					``Thus the appeal to the heavens and the earth is not merely a matter of legality; it is a matter of the whole order of life. What God's people are doing is an offense against nature. Sin, pride, and oppression are contrary to creation as God envisioned it. An ox or an ass is intelligent enough to know to whom it belongs and upon whom it can depend. But God's children are not so. Thus the stars and the earth following obediently in their courses are called to see the spectacle of thinking, feeling human beings living in ways which are contrary to their own natures.''
					
					See \hyperref[DEUTERONOMY-32-1]{Deuteronomy 32:1} and \hyperref[DEUTERONOMY-30-19]{30:19}.
					
					Note that Joseph Smith uses the same invocation in \hyperref[DC76-1]{DC 76:1}; there, however, it is not the Lord speaking but a person, and the Lord only begins to speak a bit later, in verse 5.
					}
			for the LORD hath spoken, I have nourished and brought up children, and they have rebelled against me.

			\label{ISAIAH-4-1}

			And in that day seven women shall take hold of one man, saying, We will eat our own bread, and wear our own apparel: only let us be called by thy name, to take away our reproach.

			\label{ISAIAH-4-3}

			When the Lord shall have washed away the filth of the daughters of Zion, and shall have purged the blood of Jerusalem from the midst thereof by the spirit of judgment, and by the spirit of burning.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-4-5}

			And the LORD will create upon every dwelling place of mount Zion, and upon her assemblies, a cloud and smoke by day, and the shining of a flaming fire by night: for upon all the glory shall be a defence.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-4-6}

			And there shall be a tabernacle for a shadow in the day time from the heat, and for a place of refuge, and for a covert from storm and from rain.

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 5} % *ISAIAH-5
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-5}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:1} % *ISAIAH-5-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-1}

			Now will I sing to my wellbeloved a song of my beloved touching his vineyard. My wellbeloved hath a vineyard in a very fruitful hill:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:2} % *ISAIAH-5-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-2}

			And he fenced it, and gathered out the stones thereof, and planted it with the choicest vine, and built a tower in the midst of it, and also made a winepress therein: and he looked that it should bring forth grapes, and it brought forth wild grapes.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:3} % *ISAIAH-5-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-3}

			And now, O inhabitants of Jerusalem, and men of Judah, judge, I pray you, betwixt me and my vineyard.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:4} % *ISAIAH-5-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-4}

			What could have been done more to my vineyard, that I have not done in it? wherefore, when I looked that it should bring forth grapes, brought it forth wild grapes?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:5} % *ISAIAH-5-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-5}

			And now go to; I will tell you what I will do to my vineyard: I will take away the hedge thereof, and it shall be eaten up; and break down the wall thereof, and it shall be trodden down:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:6} % *ISAIAH-5-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-6}

			And I will lay it waste: it shall not be pruned, nor digged; but there shall come up briers and thorns: I will also command the clouds that they rain no rain upon it.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:7} % *ISAIAH-5-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-7}

			For the vineyard of the LORD of hosts is the house of Israel, and the men of Judah his pleasant plant: and he looked for judgment, but behold oppression; for righteousness, but behold a cry.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:8} % *ISAIAH-5-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-8}

			Woe unto them that join house to house, that lay field to field, till there be no place, that they may be placed alone in the midst of the earth!

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:9} % *ISAIAH-5-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-9}

			In mine ears said the LORD of hosts, Of a truth many houses shall be desolate, even great and fair, without inhabitant.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:10} % *ISAIAH-5-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-10}

			Yea, ten acres of vineyard shall yield one bath, and the seed of an homer shall yield an ephah.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:11} % *ISAIAH-5-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-11}

			Woe unto them that rise up early in the morning, that they may follow strong drink; that continue until night, till wine inflame them!

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:12} % *ISAIAH-5-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-12}

			And the harp, and the viol, the tabret, and pipe, and wine, are in their feasts: but they regard not the work of the LORD, neither consider the operation of his hands.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:13} % *ISAIAH-5-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-13}

			Therefore my people are gone into captivity, because they have no knowledge: and their honourable men are famished, and their multitude dried up with thirst.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 5:14} % *ISAIAH-5-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-5-14}

			Therefore hell hath enlarged herself, and opened her mouth without measure: and their glory, and their multitude, and their pomp, and he that rejoiceth, shall descend into it.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 6} % *ISAIAH-6
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-6}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 6:1} % *ISAIAH-6-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-1}

			In the year that king Uzziah died I saw also the LORD sitting upon a throne, high and lifted up, and his train filled the temple.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 6:2} % *ISAIAH-6-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-2}

			Above it stood the seraphims: each one had six wings; with twain he covered his face, and with twain he covered his feet, and with twain he did fly.

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 6:3} % *ISAIAH-6-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-3}

			And one cried unto another, and said, Holy, holy, holy, is the LORD of hosts: the whole earth is full of his glory.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-4}

			And the posts of the door moved at the voice of him that cried, and the house was filled with smoke.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 6:5} % *ISAIAH-6-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-5}

			Then said I, Woe is me! for I am undone; because I am a man of unclean lips, and I dwell in the midst of a people of unclean lips: for mine eyes have seen the King, the LORD of hosts.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-6}

			Then flew one of the seraphims unto me, having a live coal in his hand, which he had taken with the tongs from off the altar:

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-7}

			And he laid it upon my mouth, and said, Lo, this hath touched thy lips; and thine iniquity is taken away, and thy sin purged.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-8}

			Also I heard the voice of the Lord, saying, Whom shall I send, and who will go for us? Then said I, Here am I; send me.

			% \scriptsize

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 6:9} % *ISAIAH-6-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-6-9}

			And he said, Go, and tell this people, Hear ye indeed, but understand not; and see ye indeed, but perceive not.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-9-1}

			Nevertheless the dimness shall not be such as was in her vexation, when at the first he lightly afflicted the land of Zebulun and the land of Naphtali, and afterward did more grievously afflict her by the way of the sea, beyond Jordan, in Galilee of the nations.

			For unto us a child is born, unto us a son is given: and the government shall be upon his shoulder: and his name shall be called Wonderful, Counsellor, The mighty God, The everlasting Father, The Prince of Peace.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-9-17}

			Therefore the LORD shall have no joy in their young men, neither shall have mercy on their fatherless and widows: for every one is an hypocrite and an evildoer, and every mouth speaketh folly. For all this his anger is not turned away, but his hand is stretched out still.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-9-18}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-1}

			% \scriptsize

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:2} % *ISAIAH-10-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-2}

			To turn aside the needy from judgment, and to take away the right from the poor of my people, that widows may be their prey, and that they may rob the fatherless!

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:3} % *ISAIAH-10-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-3}

			And what will ye do in the day of visitation, and in the desolation which shall come from far? to whom will ye flee for help? and where will ye leave your glory?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-4}

			Without me they shall bow down under the prisoners, and they shall fall under the slain. For all this his anger is not turned away, but his hand is stretched out still.

		% % ---

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-6}

			I will send him against an hypocritical nation, and against the people of my wrath will I give him a charge, to take the spoil, and to take the prey, and to tread them down like the mire of the streets.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-7}

			Howbeit he meaneth not so, neither doth his heart think so; but it is in his heart to destroy and cut off nations not a few.

		% % ---

			Is not Calno as Carchemish? is not Hamath as Arpad? is not Samaria as Damascus?

			Shall I not, as I have done unto Samaria and her idols, so do to Jerusalem and her idols?

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-12}

			Wherefore it shall come to pass, that when the Lord hath performed his whole work upon mount Zion and on Jerusalem, I will punish the fruit of the stout heart of the king of Assyria, and the glory of his high looks.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-13}

			For he saith, By the strength of my hand I have done it, and by my wisdom; for I am prudent: and I have removed the bounds of the people, and have robbed their treasures, and I have put down the inhabitants like a valiant man:

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:14} % *ISAIAH-10-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-14}

			And my hand hath found as a nest the riches of the people: and as one gathereth eggs that are left, have I gathered all the earth; and there was none that moved the wing, or opened the mouth, or peeped.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-15}

			Shall the axe boast itself against him that heweth therewith? or shall the saw magnify itself against him that shaketh it? as if the rod should shake itself against them that lift it up, or as if the staff should lift up itself, as if it were no wood.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-16}

			Therefore shall the Lord, the Lord of hosts, send among his fat ones leanness; and under his glory he shall kindle a burning like the burning of a fire.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-17}

			And the light of Israel shall be for a fire, and his Holy One for a flame: and it shall burn and devour his thorns and his briers in one day;

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-18}

			And shall consume the glory of his forest, and of his fruitful field, both soul and body: and they shall be as when a standard-bearer fainteth.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-19}

			And the rest of the trees of his forest shall be few, that a child may write them.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-20}

			And it shall come to pass in that day, that the remnant of Israel, and such as are escaped of the house of Jacob, shall no more again stay upon him that smote them; but shall stay upon the LORD, the Holy One of Israel, in truth.

		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:21} % *ISAIAH-10-21
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-21}

			The remnant shall return, even the remnant of Jacob, unto the mighty God.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:22} % *ISAIAH-10-22
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-22}

			For though thy people Israel be as the sand of the sea, yet a remnant of them shall return: the consumption decreed shall overflow with righteousness.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:23} % *ISAIAH-10-23
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-23}

			For the Lord GOD of hosts shall make a consumption, even determined, in the midst of all the land.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:24} % *ISAIAH-10-24
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-24}

			Therefore thus saith the Lord GOD of hosts, O my people that dwellest in Zion, be not afraid of the Assyrian: he shall smite thee with a rod, and shall lift up his staff against thee, after the manner of Egypt.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:25} % *ISAIAH-10-25
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-25}

			For yet a very little while, and the indignation shall cease, and mine anger in their destruction.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 10:26} % *ISAIAH-10-26
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-26}

			And the LORD of hosts shall stir up a scourge for him according to the slaughter of Midian at the rock of Oreb: and as his rod was upon the sea, so shall he lift it up after the manner of Egypt.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-27}

			And it shall come to pass in that day, that his burden shall be taken away from off thy shoulder, and his yoke from off thy neck, and the yoke shall be destroyed because of the anointing.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-10-28}

			He is come to Aiath, he is passed to Migron; at Michmash he hath laid up his carriages:

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 11} % *ISAIAH-11
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-11}
		
			\hyperref[DC113-1]{DC 113:1--6} is a revelatory commentary on some of the verses in this chapter, so be sure to check there.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 11:1} % *ISAIAH-11-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-11-1}

			And there shall come forth a rod out of the stem%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Hebrew \he{g*eza`}. Commenting on the use of this word in this passage, NIDOTTE 1:846 says: ``In Isaiah 11:1 the word is used as a metaphor for the Messiah: `A shoot (\he{.ho.tEr}) will come from the stump (\he{g*eza`}) of Jesse; from his roots (\he{+sorE+s}) a Branch (\he{ne.sEr}) will bear fruit.'' In the immediately preceding verses (10:33--34) the prophet has just described the forests being cut down, but from a remaining stump will sprout a Branch. Again, stump is used in the same sentence with root (\he{+sorE+s}); similar imagery is found in \hyperref[JEREMIAH-23-5]{Jeremiah 23:5}, \hyperref[JEREMIAH-33-15]{33:15}, \hyperref[ZECHARIAH-3-8]{Zechariah 3:8}. This prophecy reaches behind David to Jesse, just as \hyperref[MICAH-5-1]{Micah 5:1--2} reaches behind Jerusalem to Bethlehem, possibly to note the Messiah's humble and obscure earthly origin (cf. \hyperref[LUKE-2-7]{Luke 2:7}). The forestry imagery is picked up from Isaiah 10:33--34 and illuminates the cut back statues of the reign of Ahaz and the reduced area controlled by Judah. All people will rally around the `shoot from the stump of Jesse.' The messianic understanding of this passage was already reflected in the ancient Targum on Isaiah 11:1: `And a king shall come forth from the sons of Jesse' (B. D. Chilton, \textit{The Isaiah Targum}, 1987, 2:28).'' NIDOTTE here also notes a connection to the book of Revelation: ``\hyperref[REVELATIONNT-5-5]{Revelation 5:5} also refers to the `root of David' and \hyperref[REVELATIONNT-22-16]{Revelation 22:16} combines `Root' and `Offpsring' of David.''
					}
			of Jesse, and a Branch shall grow out of his roots:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}

			But with righteousness shall he judge the poor, and reprove with equity for the meek of the earth: and he shall smite the earth with the rod of his mouth,%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					NICOT suggests that the ``rod'' here is the word of God, as an active force; it would go along with the idea in \hyperref[JACOB-4-9]{Jacob 4:9}. NICOT says, ``the word itself becomes his weapon'' (281), and then references \hyperref[HEBREWS-4-12]{Hebrews 4:12}, \hyperref[2THESSALONIANS-2-8]{2 Thessalonians 2:8}, \hyperref[REVELATIONNT-1-16]{Revelation 1:16}, and \hyperref[REVELATIONNT-19-15]{Revelation 19:15}. ``Rod'' here is \he{+sebE.t}, ``rod, staff, branch, tribe.'' It is not the same word as ``rod'' in \hyperref[ISAIAH-11-1]{verse 1} of this chapter---that word is \he{.ho.tEr}.
					}

			\label{ISAIAH-13-4}

			The noise of a multitude in the mountains, like as of a great people; a tumultuous noise of the kingdoms of nations gathered together: the LORD of hosts mustereth the host of the battle.

			\label{ISAIAH-13-5}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-8}

			And they shall be afraid: pangs and sorrows shall take hold of them; they shall be in pain as a woman that travaileth: they shall be amazed one at another; their faces shall be as flames.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-9}

			Behold, the day of the LORD cometh, cruel both with wrath and fierce anger, to lay the land desolate: and he shall destroy the sinners thereof out of it.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-10}

			For the stars of heaven and the constellations thereof shall not give their light: the sun shall be darkened in his going forth, and the moon shall not cause her light to shine.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-11}

			And I will punish the world for their evil, and the wicked for their iniquity; and I will cause the arrogancy of the proud to cease, and will lay low the haughtiness of the terrible.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-12}

			I will make a man more precious than fine gold; even a man than the golden wedge of Ophir.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-13}

			Therefore I will shake the heavens, and the earth shall remove out of her place, in the wrath of the LORD of hosts, and in the day of his fierce anger.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 13:14} % *ISAIAH-13-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-14}

			And it shall be as the chased roe, and as a sheep that no man taketh up: they shall every man turn to his own people, and flee every one into his own land.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 13:15} % *ISAIAH-13-15
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-15}

			Every one that is found shall be thrust through; and every one that is joined unto them shall fall by the sword.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 13:16} % *ISAIAH-13-16
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-16}

			Their children also shall be dashed to pieces before their eyes; their houses shall be spoiled, and their wives ravished.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 13:17} % *ISAIAH-13-17
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-17}

			Behold, I will stir up the Medes against them, which shall not regard silver; and as for gold, they shall not delight in it.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 13:18} % *ISAIAH-13-18
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-18}

			Their bows also shall dash the young men to pieces; and they shall have no pity on the fruit of the womb; their eye shall not spare children.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-19}

			And Babylon, the glory of kingdoms, the beauty of the Chaldees' excellency, shall be as when God overthrew Sodom and Gomorrah.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 13:20} % *ISAIAH-13-20
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-13-20}

			It shall never be inhabited, neither shall it be dwelt in from generation to generation: neither shall the Arabian pitch tent there; neither shall the shepherds make their fold there.

			% \begin{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 14:12} % *ISAIAH-14-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-12}
			\label{HELEL} % See below, in the notes to the Hebrew version
			\label{HEOSPHOROS} % See below, in the notes to the LXX version

			How art thou fallen from heaven, O Lucifer, son of the morning!%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					This is the only place that the term ``Lucifer'' appears in the Bible; it shows up once in the BOM, quoting this verse (\hyperref[2NEPHI-24-12]{2 Nephi 24:12}), and once at \hyperref[DC76-26]{DC 76:26}, and that's all the uses in the scriptures. See below for notes on the Hebrew, LXX, and Vulgate versions of this verse.
					
					Based on what I've read, it looks like the term ``Lucifer'' in the KJV is an attempt to catch onto the shining-ness of \he{heylel} and also link into the ancient understanding of Venus through the term's connections to the ``morning-star'' and Venus. See the quote in NICOT below; apparently the modern interpretation is that this verse does \textit{not} refer to Satan. The use in DC 76:26, then, would be a theological addition, or a typical JS way of working, where you take a passage in the Bible and use it as the basis for building up the theology of something in a way that goes beyond the source material. Note the mention of \hyperref[LUKE-10-18]{Luke 10:18} below, which would be a natural connection to this verse, and would give some support to the traditional reading.
					
					NICOT 320--321 says: ``Some of the church fathers, linking this passage to \hyperref[LUKE-10-18]{Luke 10:18} and \hyperref[REVELATIONNT-12-8]{Revelation 12:8--9} took it to refer to the fall of Satan described in those places. However, the great expositors of the Reformation were unanimous in arguing that the context here does not support such an interpretation. This passage is discussing human pride, which, while monumental to be sure, is still human and not angelic. In fact, it is this very characteristic which makes this passage of special interest. Since the earliest discoveries of ancient Near Eastern religious literature this portion of the poem and other sections of the Bible have been compared to that literature. In this case, the vigor of this comparison was only increased by the discovery of the numerous verbal similarities with Canaanite myths uncovered at Ugarit. It is interesting that despite this vigorous investigation there is no single mythical story which can be said to be the prototype for Isa. 14:12--15. But it is of greater significance that among the numerous stories of a challenge to the high god, all the challenges are by another god. The remarkable thing about the Isaiah passage is that the challenge is by a human being. The prophet seems to be saying that ultimately the battle is not among the various manifestations of deity. Ultimately, the battle is between Creator and creatures, and the issue is whether we will accord him the right due him as Creator and bow to him in glad service or will continue to insist that we are as he is and continue to have our arrogance mocked by the worm. By alluding to the great literature of his day, drawing upon the rich connotations of those stories, Isaiah drives home the point that making God in our image is the great folly of humanity'' (320--321).
					
					``\textit{Day Star, Son of the Morning} reflects the likelihood that \he{heylel b*En--+sA.har} refers to the planet Venus, which never reaches the zenith before the sun rises and apparently extinguishes it. \he{heylel}probably comes from the root meaning `bright' and thus logically applies to the brightest star'' (321).
					
					NAC: ``The introductory `How'  in 14:12 (repeating 14:4b) marks the beginning of this new paragraph, reminding the audience that this is a lament for a dead person who has fallen (cf. the lament for Saul in 2 Sam 1:19). The lament mourns the humiliation of one who formerly enjoyed a high position. Being cast down to earth implies a loss of power, status, self-determination, and influence. The `morning star' (lit. `shining one,' \he{heylel}) probably refers to Venus, which is the `son of the dawn,' the morning star that was sometimes used to represent a divinity in ancient Near Eastern religion. This analogy indicates how high this Babylonian king had raised himself up and how far he would fall.''
				}
			how art thou cut down to the ground, which didst weaken the nations!

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[BHS] ``O Lucifer, son of the morning'' = \he{heylel b*En--+sA.har}
						\begin{compactenum}
							\item \he{heylel} comes from the root \he{hll}; see HALOT 248b (PDF 305): ``Arabic \textit{halla} to appear on the horizon, Vulgate to shine, \textit{hil\=al}, new moon $\rightarrow$ \he{heylel}.'' See also BDB 237b (PDF 256)---for \he{hAlal} they say ``\textbf{shine},'' and then for \he{heylel} they say it's an appellation, ``\textbf{shining one}, epithet of king of Babylon'' and then gloss this part in Isaiah as ``\textit{how art thou fallen, shining one, son of dawn!} i.e. star of the morning.'' DCB PDF 1037 says, for the verb, ``\textbf{shine}'' or ``\textbf{cause to shine},'' and then gives ``\textit{shining one}'' for \he{heylel}.
						\end{compactenum}
					\item[LXX] \gr{πῶς ἐξέπεσεν ἐκ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ὁ ἑωσφόρος ὁ πρωῒ ἀνατέλλων; συνετρίβη εἰς τὴν γῆν ὁ ἀποστέλλων πρὸς πάντα τὰ ἔθνη.}
						\begin{compactenum}
							\item The key word, the title for the individual, is \gr{ἑωσφόρος}. GELSM 312b says that this word is ``\textit{the morning-star}'' and notes that it appears here and \hyperref[JOB-3-9]{Job 3:9}. The second definition is ``\textit{the time of appearance of the morning-star},'' and the reference is to 1 Kings 30:17, which in speaking of the LXX is actually \hyperref[1SAMUEL-30-17]{1 Samuel 30:17}. GELSL PDF 563 says the word means ``\textit{morning star, morning},'' and also gives the reference to 1 Samuel, plus to \hyperref[PSALMS-110-3]{Psalms 110:3}. LSJ 752a (PDF 823) says that it means ``\textit{bringer of morn, the Morning-star}.'' 
						\end{compactenum}
					\item[BSV] quomodo cecidisti de caelo lucifer qui mane oriebaris corruisti in terram qui vulnerabas gentes
						\begin{compactenum}
							\item Literally translated, the Latin is more like, ``How did you fall from heaven, Lucifer, [you] who used to rise in the morning?'' At LS 1080a (PDF 1094), it says that \textit{lucifer} is an adjective meaning ``\textit{light-bringing}.'' As a substantive the word means ``\textit{the morning-star, the planet Venus}.'' 
						\end{compactenum}
					\item[ASV] How art thou fallen from heaven, O day-star, son of the morning! how art thou cut down to the ground, that didst lay low the nations!
					\item[NASB] How you have fallen from heaven, O star of the morning, son of the dawn! You have been cut down to the earth, You who have weakened the nations!
					\item[NIV] How you have fallen from heaven, morning star, son of the dawn! You have been cast down to the earth, you who once laid low the nations!
					\item[NRSV] How you are fallen from heaven, O Day Star, son of Dawn! How you are cut down to the ground, you who laid the nations low!
					\item[NICOT 319] How you have fallen from heaven,
										Day Star, Son of the Morning.
									You have been hewn down to the earth,
										weak upon the nations.
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[JUSTNT*]
					% \item[OTX*]
					% \item[OTXX*]
					% \item[OTXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXXXX*]
					% \item[NETS]
					% \item[OTR1]
					% \item[OTR2]
					% \item[OTX*]
					% \item[OTXX*]
					% \item[OTXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXX*]

		\paragraph{Isaiah 14:17} % *ISAIAH-14-17
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-17}

			That made the world as a wilderness, and destroyed the cities thereof; that opened not the house of his prisoners?

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-19}

			But thou art cast out of thy grave like an abominable branch, and as the raiment of those that are slain, thrust through with a sword, that go down to the stones of the pit; as a carcase trodden under feet.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-20}

			Thou shalt not be joined with them in burial, because thou hast destroyed thy land, and slain thy people: the seed of evildoers shall never be renowned.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-21}

			Prepare slaughter for his children for the iniquity of their fathers; that they do not rise, nor possess the land, nor fill the face of the world with cities.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-22}

			For I will rise up against them, saith the LORD of hosts, and cut off from Babylon the name, and remnant, and son, and nephew, saith the LORD.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-23}

			I will also make it a possession for the bittern, and pools of water: and I will sweep it with the besom of destruction, saith the LORD of hosts.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-25}

			That I will break the Assyrian in my land, and upon my mountains tread him under foot: then shall his yoke depart from off them, and his burden depart from off their shoulders.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-26}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-27}

		\paragraph{Isaiah 14:29} % *ISAIAH-14-29
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-29}

			Rejoice not thou, whole Palestina, because the rod of him that smote thee is broken: for out of the serpent's root shall come forth a cockatrice, and his fruit shall be a fiery flying serpent.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-30}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-31}

			Howl, O gate; cry, O city; thou, whole Palestina, art dissolved: for there shall come from the north a smoke, and none shall be alone in his appointed times.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-14-32}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 15} % *ISAIAH-15
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-15}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:1} % *ISAIAH-15-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-1}

			The burden of Moab. Because in the night Ar of Moab is laid waste, and brought to silence; because in the night Kir of Moab is laid waste, and brought to silence;

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:2} % *ISAIAH-15-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-2}

			He is gone up to Bajith, and to Dibon, the high places, to weep: Moab shall howl over Nebo, and over Medeba: on all their heads shall be baldness, and every beard cut off.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:3} % *ISAIAH-15-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-3}

			In their streets they shall gird themselves with sackcloth: on the tops of their houses, and in their streets, every one shall howl, weeping abundantly.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:4} % *ISAIAH-15-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-4}

			And Heshbon shall cry, and Elealeh: their voice shall be heard even unto Jahaz: therefore the armed soldiers of Moab shall cry out; his life shall be grievous unto him.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:5} % *ISAIAH-15-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-5}

			My heart shall cry out for Moab; his fugitives shall flee unto Zoar, an heifer of three years old: for by the mounting up of Luhith with weeping shall they go it up; for in the way of Horonaim they shall raise up a cry of destruction.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:6} % *ISAIAH-15-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-6}

			For the waters of Nimrim shall be desolate: for the hay is withered away, the grass faileth, there is no green thing.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:7} % *ISAIAH-15-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-7}

			Therefore the abundance they have gotten, and that which they have laid up, shall they carry away to the brook of the willows.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:8} % *ISAIAH-15-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-8}

			For the cry is gone round about the borders of Moab; the howling thereof unto Eglaim, and the howling thereof unto Beerelim.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 15:9} % *ISAIAH-15-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-15-9}

			For the waters of Dimon shall be full of blood: for I will bring more upon Dimon, lions upon him that escapeth of Moab, and upon the remnant of the land.

	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-16}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:1} % *ISAIAH-16-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-1}

			Send ye the lamb to the ruler of the land from Sela to the wilderness, unto the mount of the daughter of Zion.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:2} % *ISAIAH-16-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-2}

			For it shall be, that, as a wandering bird cast out of the nest, so the daughters of Moab shall be at the fords of Arnon.

			% \scriptsize

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:3} % *ISAIAH-16-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-3}

			Take counsel, execute judgment; make thy shadow as the night in the midst of the noonday; hide the outcasts; bewray not him that wandereth.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:4} % *ISAIAH-16-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-4}

			Let mine outcasts dwell with thee, Moab; be thou a covert to them from the face of the spoiler: for the extortioner is at an end, the spoiler ceaseth, the oppressors are consumed out of the land.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:5} % *ISAIAH-16-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-5}

			And in mercy shall the throne be established: and he shall sit upon it in truth in the tabernacle of David, judging, and seeking judgment, and hasting righteousness.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:6} % *ISAIAH-16-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-6}

			We have heard of the pride of Moab; he is very proud: even of his haughtiness, and his pride, and his wrath: but his lies shall not be so.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:7} % *ISAIAH-16-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-7}

			Therefore shall Moab howl for Moab, every one shall howl: for the foundations of Kirhareseth shall ye mourn; surely they are stricken.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:8} % *ISAIAH-16-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-8}

			For the fields of Heshbon languish, and the vine of Sibmah: the lords of the heathen have broken down the principal plants thereof, they are come even unto Jazer, they wandered through the wilderness: her branches are stretched out, they are gone over the sea.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:9} % *ISAIAH-16-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-9}

			Therefore I will bewail with the weeping of Jazer the vine of Sibmah: I will water thee with my tears, O Heshbon, and Elealeh: for the shouting for thy summer fruits and for thy harvest is fallen.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:10} % *ISAIAH-16-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-10}

			And gladness is taken away, and joy out of the plentiful field; and in the vineyards there shall be no singing, neither shall there be shouting: the treaders shall tread out no wine in their presses; I have made their vintage shouting to cease.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:11} % *ISAIAH-16-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-11}

			Wherefore my bowels shall sound like an harp for Moab, and mine inward parts for Kirharesh.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:12} % *ISAIAH-16-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-12}

			And it shall come to pass, when it is seen that Moab is weary on the high place, that he shall come to his sanctuary to pray; but he shall not prevail.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:13} % *ISAIAH-16-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-13}

			This is the word that the LORD hath spoken concerning Moab since that time.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 16:14} % *ISAIAH-16-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-16-14}

			But now the LORD hath spoken, saying, Within three years, as the years of an hireling, and the glory of Moab shall be contemned, with all that great multitude; and the remnant shall be very small and feeble.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 17} % *ISAIAH-17
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-17}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:1} % *ISAIAH-17-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-1}

			The burden of Damascus. Behold, Damascus is taken away from being a city, and it shall be a ruinous heap.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:2} % *ISAIAH-17-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-2}

			The cities of Aroer are forsaken: they shall be for flocks, which shall lie down, and none shall make them afraid.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:3} % *ISAIAH-17-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-3}

			The fortress also shall cease from Ephraim, and the kingdom from Damascus, and the remnant of Syria: they shall be as the glory of the children of Israel, saith the LORD of hosts.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:4} % *ISAIAH-17-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-4}

			And in that day it shall come to pass, that the glory of Jacob shall be made thin, and the fatness of his flesh shall wax lean.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:5} % *ISAIAH-17-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-5}

			And it shall be as when the harvestman gathereth the corn, and reapeth the ears with his arm; and it shall be as he that gathereth ears in the valley of Rephaim.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:6} % *ISAIAH-17-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-6}

			Yet gleaning grapes shall be left in it, as the shaking of an olive tree, two or three berries in the top of the uppermost bough, four or five in the outmost fruitful branches thereof, saith the LORD God of Israel.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:7} % *ISAIAH-17-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-7}

			At that day shall a man look to his Maker, and his eyes shall have respect to the Holy One of Israel.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:8} % *ISAIAH-17-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-8}

			And he shall not look to the altars, the work of his hands, neither shall respect that which his fingers have made, either the groves, or the images.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:9} % *ISAIAH-17-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-9}

			In that day shall his strong cities be as a forsaken bough, and an uppermost branch, which they left because of the children of Israel: and there shall be desolation.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTR1]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:10} % *ISAIAH-17-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-10}

			Because thou hast forgotten the God of thy salvation, and hast not been mindful of the rock of thy strength, therefore shalt thou plant pleasant plants, and shalt set it with strange slips:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTR1]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:11} % *ISAIAH-17-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-11}

			In the day shalt thou make thy plant to grow, and in the morning shalt thou make thy seed to flourish: but the harvest shall be a heap in the day of grief and of desperate sorrow.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]

					% % \item[OTXX*]

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-12}

			Woe to the multitude of many people, which make a noise like the noise of the seas; and to the rushing of nations, that make a rushing like the rushing of mighty waters!

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:13} % *ISAIAH-17-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-13}

			The nations shall rush like the rushing of many waters: but God shall rebuke them, and they shall flee far off, and shall be chased as the chaff of the mountains before the wind, and like a rolling thing before the whirlwind.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 17:14} % *ISAIAH-17-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-17-14}

			And behold at eveningtide trouble; and before the morning he is not. This is the portion of them that spoil us, and the lot of them that rob us.

		% % ---

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-18-2}

			That sendeth ambassadors by the sea, even in vessels of bulrushes upon the waters, saying, Go, ye swift messengers, to a nation scattered and peeled, to a people terrible from their beginning hitherto; a nation meted out and trodden down, whose land the rivers have spoiled!

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-18-3}

			All ye inhabitants of the world, and dwellers on the earth, see ye, when he lifteth up an ensign on the mountains; and when he bloweth a trumpet, hear ye.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-18-4}

			For so the LORD said unto me, I will take my rest, and I will consider in my dwelling place like a clear heat upon herbs, and like a cloud of dew in the heat of harvest.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-18-5}

			For afore the harvest, when the bud is perfect, and the sour grape is ripening in the flower, he shall both cut off the sprigs with pruning hooks, and take away and cut down the branches.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-18-6}

			They shall be left together unto the fowls of the mountains, and to the beasts of the earth: and the fowls shall summer upon them, and all the beasts of the earth shall winter upon them.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-18-7}

			In that time shall the present be brought unto the LORD of hosts of a people scattered and peeled, and from a people terrible from their beginning hitherto; a nation meted out and trodden under foot, whose land the rivers have spoiled, to the place of the name of the LORD of hosts, the mount Zion.

	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-19}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:1} % *ISAIAH-19-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-1}

			The burden of Egypt. Behold, the LORD rideth upon a swift cloud, and shall come into Egypt: and the idols of Egypt shall be moved at his presence, and the heart of Egypt shall melt in the midst of it.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:2} % *ISAIAH-19-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-2}

			And I will set the Egyptians against the Egyptians: and they shall fight every one against his brother, and every one against his neighbour; city against city, and kingdom against kingdom.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:3} % *ISAIAH-19-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-3}

			And the spirit of Egypt shall fail in the midst thereof; and I will destroy the counsel thereof: and they shall seek to the idols, and to the charmers, and to them that have familiar spirits, and to the wizards.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:4} % *ISAIAH-19-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-4}

			And the Egyptians will I give over into the hand of a cruel lord; and a fierce king shall rule over them, saith the Lord, the LORD of hosts.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:5} % *ISAIAH-19-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-5}

			And the waters shall fail from the sea, and the river shall be wasted and dried up.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:6} % *ISAIAH-19-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-6}

			And they shall turn the rivers far away; and the brooks of defence shall be emptied and dried up: the reeds and flags shall wither.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:7} % *ISAIAH-19-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-7}

			The paper reeds by the brooks, by the mouth of the brooks, and every thing sown by the brooks, shall wither, be driven away, and be no more.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:8} % *ISAIAH-19-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-8}

			The fishers also shall mourn, and all they that cast angle into the brooks shall lament, and they that spread nets upon the waters shall languish.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:9} % *ISAIAH-19-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-9}

			Moreover they that work in fine flax, and they that weave networks, shall be confounded.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:10} % *ISAIAH-19-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-10}

			And they shall be broken in the purposes thereof, all that make sluices and ponds for fish.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:11} % *ISAIAH-19-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-11}

			Surely the princes of Zoan are fools, the counsel of the wise counsellors of Pharaoh is become brutish: how say ye unto Pharaoh, I am the son of the wise, the son of ancient kings?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:12} % *ISAIAH-19-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-12}

			Where are they? where are thy wise men? and let them tell thee now, and let them know what the LORD of hosts hath purposed upon Egypt.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:13} % *ISAIAH-19-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-13}

			The princes of Zoan are become fools, the princes of Noph are deceived; they have also seduced Egypt, even they that are the stay of the tribes thereof.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:14} % *ISAIAH-19-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-14}

			The LORD hath mingled a perverse spirit in the midst thereof: and they have caused Egypt to err in every work thereof, as a drunken man staggereth in his vomit.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 19:15} % *ISAIAH-19-15
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-15}

			Neither shall there be any work for Egypt, which the head or tail, branch or rush, may do.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-16}

			In that day shall Egypt be like unto women: and it shall be afraid and fear because of the shaking of the hand of the LORD of hosts, which he shaketh over it.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-17}

			And the land of Judah shall be a terror unto Egypt, every one that maketh mention thereof shall be afraid in himself, because of the counsel of the LORD of hosts, which he hath determined against it.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-19}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-20}

			And it shall be for a sign and for a witness unto the LORD of hosts in the land of Egypt: for they shall cry unto the LORD because of the oppressors, and he shall send them a saviour, and a great one, and he shall deliver them.

			\label{ISAIAH-19-21}

			And the LORD shall be known to Egypt, and the Egyptians shall know the LORD in that day, and shall do sacrifice and oblation; yea, they shall vow a vow unto the LORD, and perform it.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-19-22}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 20} % *ISAIAH-20
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-20}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 20:1} % *ISAIAH-20-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-20-1}

			In the year that Tartan came unto Ashdod, (when Sargon the king of Assyria sent him,) and fought against Ashdod, and took it;

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 20:2} % *ISAIAH-20-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-20-2}

			At the same time spake the LORD by Isaiah the son of Amoz, saying, Go and loose the sackcloth from off thy loins, and put off thy shoe from thy foot. And he did so, walking naked and barefoot.

			% \begin{framed}

				% \begin{compactdesc}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 20:3} % *ISAIAH-20-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-20-3}

			And the LORD said, Like as my servant Isaiah hath walked naked and barefoot three years for a sign and wonder upon Egypt and upon Ethiopia;

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 20:4} % *ISAIAH-20-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-20-4}

			So shall the king of Assyria lead away the Egyptians prisoners, and the Ethiopians captives, young and old, naked and barefoot, even with their buttocks uncovered, to the shame of Egypt.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 20:5} % *ISAIAH-20-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-20-5}

			And they shall be afraid and ashamed of Ethiopia their expectation, and of Egypt their glory.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 20:6} % *ISAIAH-20-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-20-6}

			And the inhabitant of this isle shall say in that day, Behold, such is our expectation, whither we flee for help to be delivered from the king of Assyria: and how shall we escape?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 21} % *ISAIAH-21
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-21}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 21:1} % *ISAIAH-21-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-1}

			The burden of the desert of the sea. As whirlwinds in the south pass through; so it cometh from the desert, from a terrible land.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 21:2} % *ISAIAH-21-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-2}

			A grievous vision is declared unto me; the treacherous dealer dealeth treacherously, and the spoiler spoileth. Go up, O Elam: besiege, O Media; all the sighing thereof have I made to cease.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 21:3} % *ISAIAH-21-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-3}

			Therefore are my loins filled with pain: pangs have taken hold upon me, as the pangs of a woman that travaileth: I was bowed down at the hearing of it; I was dismayed at the seeing of it.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 21:4} % *ISAIAH-21-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-4}

			My heart panted, fearfulness affrighted me: the night of my pleasure hath he turned into fear unto me.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 21:5} % *ISAIAH-21-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-5}

			Prepare the table, watch in the watchtower, eat, drink: arise, ye princes, and anoint the shield.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-6}

			For thus hath the LORD said unto me, Go, set a watchman, let him declare what he seeth.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-7}

			And he saw a chariot with a couple of horsemen, a chariot of asses, and a chariot of camels; and he hearkened diligently with much heed:

			% \begin{framed}

				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 21:8} % *ISAIAH-21-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-8}

			And he cried, A lion: My lord, I stand continually upon the watchtower in the daytime, and I am set in my ward whole nights:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 21:9} % *ISAIAH-21-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-21-9}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-11}

			Ye made also a ditch between the two walls for the water of the old pool: but ye have not looked unto the maker thereof, neither had respect unto him that fashioned it long ago.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-12}

			And in that day did the Lord GOD of hosts call to weeping, and to mourning, and to baldness, and to girding with sackcloth:

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-13}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-14}

			And it was revealed in mine ears by the LORD of hosts, Surely this iniquity shall not be purged from you till ye die, saith the Lord GOD of hosts.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-16}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-17}

			Behold, the LORD will carry thee away with a mighty captivity, and will surely cover thee.

			% \scriptsize

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-18}

			He will surely violently turn and toss thee like a ball into a large country: there shalt thou die, and there the chariots of thy glory shall be the shame of thy lord's house.

			\label{ISAIAH-22-21}

			And I will clothe him with thy robe, and strengthen him with thy girdle, and I will commit thy government into his hand: and he shall be a father to the inhabitants of Jerusalem, and to the house of Judah.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-22}

			And the key of the house of David will I lay upon his shoulder; so he shall open, and none shall shut; and he shall shut, and none shall open.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-23}

			And I will fasten him as a nail in a sure place; and he shall be for a glorious throne to his father's house.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-24}

			And they shall hang upon him all the glory of his father's house, the offspring and the issue, all vessels of small quantity, from the vessels of cups, even to all the vessels of flagons.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-22-25}

			In that day, saith the LORD of hosts, shall the nail that is fastened in the sure place be removed, and be cut down, and fall; and the burden that was upon it shall be cut off: for the LORD hath spoken it.

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 23} % *ISAIAH-23
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-23}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:1} % *ISAIAH-23-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-1}

			The burden of Tyre. Howl, ye ships of Tarshish; for it is laid waste, so that there is no house, no entering in: from the land of Chittim it is revealed to them.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:2} % *ISAIAH-23-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-2}

			Be still, ye inhabitants of the isle; thou whom the merchants of Zidon, that pass over the sea, have replenished.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:3} % *ISAIAH-23-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-3}

			And by great waters the seed of Sihor, the harvest of the river, is her revenue; and she is a mart of nations.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:4} % *ISAIAH-23-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-4}

			Be thou ashamed, O Zidon: for the sea hath spoken, even the strength of the sea, saying, I travail not, nor bring forth children, neither do I nourish up young men, nor bring up virgins.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:5} % *ISAIAH-23-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-5}

			As at the report concerning Egypt, so shall they be sorely pained at the report of Tyre.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:6} % *ISAIAH-23-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-6}

			Pass ye over to Tarshish; howl, ye inhabitants of the isle.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:7} % *ISAIAH-23-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-7}

			Is this your joyous city, whose antiquity is of ancient days? her own feet shall carry her afar off to sojourn.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:8} % *ISAIAH-23-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-8}

			Who hath taken this counsel against Tyre, the crowning city, whose merchants are princes, whose traffickers are the honourable of the earth?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:9} % *ISAIAH-23-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-9}

			The LORD of hosts hath purposed it, to stain the pride of all glory, and to bring into contempt all the honourable of the earth.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:10} % *ISAIAH-23-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-10}

			Pass through thy land as a river, O daughter of Tarshish: there is no more strength.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:11} % *ISAIAH-23-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-11}

			He stretched out his hand over the sea, he shook the kingdoms: the LORD hath given a commandment against the merchant city, to destroy the strong holds thereof.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:12} % *ISAIAH-23-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-12}

			And he said, Thou shalt no more rejoice, O thou oppressed virgin, daughter of Zidon: arise, pass over to Chittim; there also shalt thou have no rest.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:13} % *ISAIAH-23-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-13}

			Behold the land of the Chaldeans; this people was not, till the Assyrian founded it for them that dwell in the wilderness: they set up the towers thereof, they raised up the palaces thereof; and he brought it to ruin.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:14} % *ISAIAH-23-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-14}

			Howl, ye ships of Tarshish: for your strength is laid waste.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:15} % *ISAIAH-23-15
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-15}

			And it shall come to pass in that day, that Tyre shall be forgotten seventy years, according to the days of one king: after the end of seventy years shall Tyre sing as an harlot.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:16} % *ISAIAH-23-16
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-16}

			Take an harp, go about the city, thou harlot that hast been forgotten; make sweet melody, sing many songs, that thou mayest be remembered.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 23:17} % *ISAIAH-23-17
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-23-17}

			And it shall come to pass after the end of seventy years, that the LORD will visit Tyre, and she shall turn to her hire, and shall commit fornication with all the kingdoms of the world upon the face of the earth.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			And her merchandise and her hire shall be holiness to the LORD: it shall not be treasured nor laid up; for her merchandise shall be for them that dwell before the LORD, to eat sufficiently, and for durable clothing.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-24-1}

			Behold, the LORD maketh the earth empty, and maketh it waste, and turneth it upside down, and scattereth abroad the inhabitants thereof.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-24-2}

			And it shall be, as with the people, so with the priest; as with the servant, so with his master; as with the maid, so with her mistress; as with the buyer, so with the seller; as with the lender, so with the borrower; as with the taker of usury, so with the giver of usury to him.

			\label{ISAIAH-24-16}

			From the uttermost part of the earth have we heard songs, even glory to the righteous. But I said, My leanness, my leanness, woe unto me! the treacherous dealers have dealt treacherously; yea, the treacherous dealers have dealt very treacherously.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-24-18}

			And it shall come to pass, that he who fleeth from the noise of the fear shall fall into the pit; and he that cometh up out of the midst of the pit shall be taken in the snare: for the windows from on high are open, and the foundations of the earth do shake.

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 25} % *ISAIAH-25
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-25}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 25:1} % *ISAIAH-25-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-1}

			O Lord, thou art my God; I will exalt thee, I will praise thy name; for thou hast done wonderful things; thy counsels of old are faithfulness and truth.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-2}

			For thou hast made of a city an heap; of a defenced city a ruin: a palace of strangers to be no city; it shall never be built.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-3}

			Therefore shall the strong people glorify thee, the city of the terrible nations shall fear thee.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 25:4} % *ISAIAH-25-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-4}

			For thou hast been a strength to the poor, a strength to the needy in his distress, a refuge from the storm, a shadow from the heat, when the blast of the terrible ones is as a storm against the wall.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 25:5} % *ISAIAH-25-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-5}

			Thou shalt bring down the noise of strangers, as the heat in a dry place; even the heat with the shadow of a cloud: the branch of the terrible ones shall be brought low.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 25:6} % *ISAIAH-25-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-6}

			And in this mountain shall the LORD of hosts make unto all people a feast of fat things, a feast of wines on the lees, of fat things full of marrow, of wines on the lees well refined.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-7}

			And he will destroy in this mountain the face of the covering cast over all people, and the vail that is spread over all nations.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 25:8} % *ISAIAH-25-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-8}

			He will swallow up death in victory; and the Lord GOD will wipe away tears from off all faces; and the rebuke of his people shall he take away from off all the earth: for the LORD hath spoken it.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-9}

			And it shall be said in that day, Lo, this is our God; we have waited for him, and he will save us: this is the LORD; we have waited for him, we will be glad and rejoice in his salvation.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 25:10} % *ISAIAH-25-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-10}

			For in this mountain shall the hand of the LORD rest, and Moab shall be trodden down under him, even as straw is trodden down for the dunghill.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-11}

			And he shall spread forth his hands in the midst of them, as he that swimmeth spreadeth forth his hands to swim: and he shall bring down their pride together with the spoils of their hands.

		\paragraph{Isaiah 25:12} % *ISAIAH-25-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-25-12}

			And the fortress of the high fort of thy walls shall he bring down, lay low, and bring to the ground, even to the dust.

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 26} % *ISAIAH-26
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-26}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:1} % *ISAIAH-26-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-1}

			In that day shall this song be sung in the land of Judah; We have a strong city; salvation will God appoint for walls and bulwarks.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:2} % *ISAIAH-26-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-2}

			Open ye the gates, that the righteous nation which keepeth the truth may enter in.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:3} % *ISAIAH-26-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-3}

			Thou wilt keep him in perfect peace, whose mind is stayed on thee: because he trusteth in thee.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:4} % *ISAIAH-26-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-4}

			Trust ye in the LORD for ever: for in the LORD JEHOVAH is everlasting strength:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:5} % *ISAIAH-26-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-5}

			For he bringeth down them that dwell on high; the lofty city, he layeth it low; he layeth it low, even to the ground; he bringeth it even to the dust.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:6} % *ISAIAH-26-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-6}

			The foot shall tread it down, even the feet of the poor, and the steps of the needy.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:7} % *ISAIAH-26-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-7}

			The way of the just is uprightness: thou, most upright, dost weigh the path of the just.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
