					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:8} % *ISAIAH-26-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-8}

			Yea, in the way of thy judgments, O LORD, have we waited for thee; the desire of our soul is to thy name, and to the remembrance of thee.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:9} % *ISAIAH-26-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-9}

			With my soul have I desired thee in the night; yea, with my spirit within me will I seek thee early: for when thy judgments are in the earth, the inhabitants of the world will learn righteousness.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:10} % *ISAIAH-26-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-10}

			Let favour be shewed to the wicked, yet will he not learn righteousness: in the land of uprightness will he deal unjustly, and will not behold the majesty of the LORD.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:11} % *ISAIAH-26-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-11}

			LORD, when thy hand is lifted up, they will not see: but they shall see, and be ashamed for their envy at the people; yea, the fire of thine enemies shall devour them.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:12} % *ISAIAH-26-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-12}

			LORD, thou wilt ordain peace for us: for thou also hast wrought all our works in us.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTR1]

					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:13} % *ISAIAH-26-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-13}

			O LORD our God, other lords beside thee have had dominion over us: but by thee only will we make mention of thy name.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 26:14} % *ISAIAH-26-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-14}

			They are dead, they shall not live; they are deceased, they shall not rise: therefore hast thou visited and destroyed them, and made all their memory to perish.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-15}

			Thou hast increased the nation, O LORD, thou hast increased the nation: thou art glorified: thou hadst removed it far unto all the ends of the earth.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-16}

			LORD, in trouble have they visited thee, they poured out a prayer when thy chastening was upon them.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-17}

			Like as a woman with child, that draweth near the time of her delivery, is in pain, and crieth out in her pangs; so have we been in thy sight, O LORD.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-18}

			We have been with child, we have been in pain, we have as it were brought forth wind; we have not wrought any deliverance in the earth; neither have the inhabitants of the world fallen.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-19}

			Thy dead men shall live, together with my dead body shall they arise. Awake and sing, ye that dwell in dust: for thy dew is as the dew of herbs, and the earth shall cast out the dead.

		% % ---

			Come, my people, enter thou into thy chambers, and shut thy doors about thee: hide thyself as it were for a little moment, until the indignation be overpast.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-26-21}

			For, behold, the LORD cometh out of his place to punish the inhabitants of the earth for their iniquity: the earth also shall disclose her blood, and shall no more cover her slain.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-1}

			In that day the LORD with his sore and great and strong sword shall punish leviathan the piercing serpent, even leviathan that crooked serpent; and he shall slay the dragon that is in the sea.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-3}

			I the LORD do keep it; I will water it every moment: lest any hurt it, I will keep it night and day.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-4}

			Fury is not in me: who would set the briers and thorns against me in battle? I would go through them, I would burn them together.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-5}

			Or let him take hold of my strength, that he may make peace with me; and he shall make peace with me.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-6}

			He shall cause them that come of Jacob to take root: Israel shall blossom and bud, and fill the face of the world with fruit.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 27:7} % *ISAIAH-27-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-7}

			Hath he smitten him, as he smote those that smote him? or is he slain according to the slaughter of them that are slain by him?

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 27:8} % *ISAIAH-27-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-8}

			In measure, when it shooteth forth, thou wilt debate with it: he stayeth his rough wind in the day of the east wind.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 27:9} % *ISAIAH-27-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-9}

			By this therefore shall the iniquity of Jacob be purged; and this is all the fruit to take away his sin; when he maketh all the stones of the altar as chalkstones that are beaten in sunder, the groves and images shall not stand up.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 27:10} % *ISAIAH-27-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-10}

			Yet the defenced city shall be desolate, and the habitation forsaken, and left like a wilderness: there shall the calf feed, and there shall he lie down, and consume the branches thereof.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 27:11} % *ISAIAH-27-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-11}

			When the boughs thereof are withered, they shall be broken off: the women come, and set them on fire: for it is a people of no understanding: therefore he that made them will not have mercy on them, and he that formed them will shew them no favour.

				% \begin{compactdesc}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 27:12} % *ISAIAH-27-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-12}

			And it shall come to pass in that day, that the LORD shall beat off from the channel of the river unto the stream of Egypt, and ye shall be gathered one by one, O ye children of Israel.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 27:13} % *ISAIAH-27-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-27-13}

			And it shall come to pass in that day, that the great trumpet shall be blown, and they shall come which were ready to perish in the land of Assyria, and the outcasts in the land of Egypt, and shall worship the LORD in the holy mount at Jerusalem.

			% \begin{framed}

				% \begin{compactdesc}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 28} % *ISAIAH-28
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-28}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 28:1} % *ISAIAH-28-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-28-1}

			Woe to the crown of pride, to the drunkards of Ephraim, whose glorious beauty is a fading flower, which are on the head of the fat valleys of them that are overcome with wine!

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 28:2} % *ISAIAH-28-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-28-2}

			Behold, the Lord hath a mighty and strong one, which as a tempest of hail and a destroying storm, as a flood of mighty waters overflowing, shall cast down to the earth with the hand.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 28:3} % *ISAIAH-28-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-28-3}

			The crown of pride, the drunkards of Ephraim, shall be trodden under feet:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 28:4} % *ISAIAH-28-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-28-4}

			And the glorious beauty, which is on the head of the fat valley, shall be a fading flower, and as the hasty fruit before the summer; which when he that looketh upon it seeth, while it is yet in his hand he eateth it up.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTR1]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 28:5} % *ISAIAH-28-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-28-5}

			In that day shall the LORD of hosts be for a crown of glory, and for a diadem of beauty, unto the residue of his people,

		\paragraph{Isaiah 28:10} % *ISAIAH-28-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-28-10}

			For precept must be upon precept, precept upon precept; line upon line, line upon line; here a little, and there a little:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[BHS] \he{k*iy .saw lA.sAw .saw lA.sAw qaw lAqAw qaw lAqAw z:`eyr +sAM z:`eyr +sAM}
					\item[LXX] \gr{θλῖψιν ἐπὶ θλῖψιν προσδέχου, ἐλπίδα ἐπ' ἐλπίδι, ἔτι µικρὸν ἔτι µικρὸν}
					\item[BSV] quia manda remanda manda remanda expecta reexpecta expecta reexpecta modicum ibi modicum ibi
					\item[ASV] For it is precept upon precept, precept upon precept; line upon line, line upon line; here a little, there a little.
					\item[BBE] For it is one rule after another; one line after another; here a little, there a little.
					\item[CEB] It is ``tsav letsav, tsav lestav; qav leqav, qav leqav,'' a little of this, a little of that.
					\item[HCSB] For [he says]: ``Law after law, law after law, line after line, line after line, a little here, a little there.''
					\item[LEB] For [it is] blah-blah upon blah-blah, blah-blah upon blah-blah, gah-gah upon gah-gah, gah-gah upon gah-gah, a little here, a little there.
					\item[TMB] `Da, da, da, da, blah, blah, blah, blah. That's a good little girl, that's a good little boy.'''
					\item[NASB] ``For He says, `Order on order, order on order, Line on line, line on line, A little here, a little there.'''
					\item[NCV] So they make fun of the Lord's prophet and say: ``A command here, a command there. A rule here, a rule there. A little lesson here, a little lesson there.''
					\item[NIRV] Here is how he teaches. Do this and do that. Do that and do this. Obey this rule and obey that rule. Obey that rule and obey this rule. Learn a little here and learn a little there.''
					\item[NIV] For it is: Do this, do that, a rule for this, a rule for that ; a little here, a little there.''
					\item[NLT] He tells us everything over and over--- one line at a time, one line at a time, a little here, and a little there!''
					\item[NRSV] For it is precept upon precept, precept upon precept, line upon line, line upon line, here a little, there a little.''

					\item[NETS] Expect affliction upon affliction, hope upon hope, yet a little, yet a little,

		\paragraph{Isaiah 28:13} % *ISAIAH-28-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-28-13}

			But the word of the LORD was unto them precept upon precept, precept upon precept; line upon line, line upon line; here a little, and there a little; that they might go, and fall backward, and be broken, and snared, and taken.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[BHS] \he{w:hAyAh lAhEM d*:bar--y:hwAh .saw lA.sAw .saw lA.sAw qaw lAqAw qaw lAqAw z:`eyr +sAM z:`eyr +sAM l:ma`aN yel:kU w:kA+s:lU 'A.hOr w:ni+s:b*ArU w:nOq:+sU w:nil:k*AdU}
					\item[LXX] \gr{καὶ ἔσται αὐτοῖς τὸ λόγιον κυρίου τοῦ θεοῦ θλῖψις ἐπὶ θλῖψιν, ἐλπὶς ἐπ' ἐλπίδι, ἔτι µικρὸν ἔτι µικρόν, ἵνα πορευθῶσιν καὶ πέσωσιν εἰς τὰ ὀπίσω καὶ κινδυνεύσουσιν καὶ συντριβήσονται καὶ ἁλώσονται. —}
					\item[BSV] et erit eis verbum Domini manda remanda manda remanda expecta reexpecta expecta reexpecta modicum ibi modicum ibi ut vadant et cadant retrorsum et conterantur et inlaqueentur et capiantur
					\item[ASV] Therefore shall the word of Jehovah be unto them precept upon precept, precept upon precept; line upon line, line upon line; here a little, there a little; that they may go, and fall backward, and be broken, and snared, and taken.
					\item[BBE] For this cause the word of the Lord will be to them rule after rule, line after line, here a little, there a little; so that they may go on their way, and falling back may be broken, and taken in the net.
					\item[CEB] So the LORD's word will be for them: ``tsav letsav, tsav letsav; qav leqav, qav leqav,'' a little of this, a little of that. So that they will go and stagger backward, they will be broken, snared, and captured.
					\item[HCSB] Then the word of the Lord came to them: ``Law after law, law after law, line after line, line after line, a little here, a little there,'' so they go stumbling backwards, to be broken, trapped, and captured.
					\item[LEB] And to them the word of Yahweh will be blah-blah upon blah-blah blah-blah upon blah-blah gah-gah upon gah-gah gah-gah upon gah-gah, a little here, a little there, so that they may go and stumble backward and be broken and ensnared and captured.
					\item[TMB] So God will start over with the simple basics and address them in baby talk, one syllable at a time - ``Da, da, da, da, blah, blah, blah, blah. That's a good little girl, that's a good little boy.'' And like toddlers, they will get up and fall down, get bruised and confused and lost.
					\item[NASB] So the word of the LORD to them will be, ``Order on order, order on order, Line on line, line on line, A little here, a little there,'' That they may go and stumble backward, be broken, snared and taken captive.
					\item[NCV] So the words of the Lord will be, ``A command here, a command there. A rule here, a rule there. A little lesson here, a little lesson there.'' They will fall back and be defeated; they will be trapped and captured.
					\item[NIRV] So then, here is what the LORD's message will become to them. Do this and do that. Do that and do this. Obey this rule and obey that rule. Obey that rule and obey this rule. Learn a little here and learn a little there. So when they try to go forward, they'll fall back and be wounded. They'll be trapped and captured.
					\item[NIV] So then, the word of the LORD to them will become: Do this, do that, a rule for this, a rule for that; a little here, a little there--- so that as they go they will fall backward; they will be injured and snared and captured.
					\item[NLT] So the LORD will spell out his message for them again, one line at a time, one line at a time, a little here, and a little there, so that they will stumble and fall. They will be injured, trapped, and captured.
					\item[NRSV] Therefore the word of the Lord will be to them, ``Precept upon precept, precept upon precept, line upon line, line upon line, here a little, there a little;'' in order that they may go, and fall backward, and be broken, and snared, and taken.
					% \item[AB ]
					\item[NETS] And the oracle of the Lord God will be to them affliction upon affliction, hope upon hope, yet a little, yet a little, in order that they may go and fall backward, and they will be in danger and crushed and taken.
					\item[OTR1] ---
					\item[OTR2] ---

					% \item[OTXX*]
					% \item[OTXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-1}

			Woe to Ariel, to Ariel, the city where David dwelt! add ye year to year; let them kill sacrifices.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-2}

			Yet I will distress Ariel, and there shall be heaviness and sorrow: and it shall be unto me as Ariel.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-3}

			And I will camp against thee round about, and will lay siege against thee with a mount, and I will raise forts against thee.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:4} % *ISAIAH-29-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-4}

			And thou shalt be brought down, and shalt speak out of the ground, and thy speech shall be low out of the dust, and thy voice shall be, as of one that hath a familiar spirit, out of the ground, and thy speech shall whisper out of the dust.

			% \scriptsize

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:5} % *ISAIAH-29-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-5}

			Moreover the multitude of thy strangers shall be like small dust, and the multitude of the terrible ones shall be as chaff that passeth away: yea, it shall be at an instant suddenly.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-6}

			Thou shalt be visited of the LORD of hosts with thunder, and with earthquake, and great noise, with storm and tempest, and the flame of devouring fire.

				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:7} % *ISAIAH-29-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-7}

			And the multitude of all the nations that fight against Ariel, even all that fight against her and her munition, and that distress her, shall be as a dream of a night vision.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:8} % *ISAIAH-29-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-8}

			It shall even be as when an hungry man dreameth, and, behold, he eateth; but he awaketh, and his soul is empty: or as when a thirsty man dreameth, and, behold, he drinketh; but he awaketh, and, behold, he is faint, and his soul hath appetite: so shall the multitude of all the nations be, that fight against mount Zion.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:9} % *ISAIAH-29-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-9}

			Stay yourselves, and wonder; cry ye out, and cry: they are drunken, but not with wine; they stagger, but not with strong drink.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:10} % *ISAIAH-29-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-10}

			For the LORD hath poured out upon you the spirit of deep sleep, and hath closed your eyes: the prophets and your rulers, the seers hath he covered.

			% \scriptsize

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-11}

			And the vision of all is become unto you as the words of a book that is sealed, which men deliver to one that is learned, saying, Read this, I pray thee: and he saith, I cannot; for it is sealed:

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-12}

			And the book is delivered to him that is not learned, saying, Read this, I pray thee: and he saith, I am not learned.

		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:13} % *ISAIAH-29-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-13}

			Wherefore the Lord said, Forasmuch as this people draw near me with their mouth, and with their lips do honour me, but have removed their heart far from me, and their fear toward me is taught by the precept of men:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:14} % *ISAIAH-29-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-14}

			Therefore, behold, I will proceed to do a marvellous work among this people, even a marvellous work and a wonder: for the wisdom of their wise men shall perish, and the understanding of their prudent men shall be hid.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:15} % *ISAIAH-29-15
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-15}

			Woe unto them that seek deep to hide their counsel from the LORD, and their works are in the dark, and they say, Who seeth us? and who knoweth us?

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:16} % *ISAIAH-29-16
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-16}

			Surely your turning of things upside down shall be esteemed as the potter's clay: for shall the work say of him that made it, He made me not? or shall the thing framed say of him that framed it, He had no understanding?

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:17} % *ISAIAH-29-17
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-17}

			Is it not yet a very little while, and Lebanon shall be turned into a fruitful field, and the fruitful field shall be esteemed as a forest?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-18}

			And in that day shall the deaf hear the words of the book, and the eyes of the blind shall see out of obscurity, and out of darkness.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-19}

			The meek also shall increase their joy in the LORD, and the poor among men shall rejoice in the Holy One of Israel.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-20}

			For the terrible one is brought to nought, and the scorner is consumed, and all that watch for iniquity are cut off:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:21} % *ISAIAH-29-21
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-21}

			That make a man an offender for a word, and lay a snare for him that reproveth in the gate, and turn aside the just for a thing of nought.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-22}

			Therefore thus saith the LORD, who redeemed Abraham, concerning the house of Jacob, Jacob shall not now be ashamed, neither shall his face now wax pale.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:23} % *ISAIAH-29-23
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-23}

			But when he seeth his children, the work of mine hands, in the midst of him, they shall sanctify my name, and sanctify the Holy One of Jacob, and shall fear the God of Israel.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 29:24} % *ISAIAH-29-24
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-29-24}

			They also that erred in spirit shall come to understanding, and they that murmured shall learn doctrine.

	% -----
	\subsubsection{Isaiah 30} % *ISAIAH-30
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-30}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:1} % *ISAIAH-30-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-1}

			Woe to the rebellious children, saith the LORD, that take counsel, but not of me; and that cover with a covering, but not of my spirit, that they may add sin to sin:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:2} % *ISAIAH-30-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-2}

			That walk to go down into Egypt, and have not asked at my mouth; to strengthen themselves in the strength of Pharaoh, and to trust in the shadow of Egypt!

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:3} % *ISAIAH-30-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-3}

			Therefore shall the strength of Pharaoh be your shame, and the trust in the shadow of Egypt your confusion.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:4} % *ISAIAH-30-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-4}

			For his princes were at Zoan, and his ambassadors came to Hanes.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:5} % *ISAIAH-30-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-5}

			They were all ashamed of a people that could not profit them, nor be an help nor profit, but a shame, and also a reproach.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:6} % *ISAIAH-30-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-6}

			The burden of the beasts of the south: into the land of trouble and anguish, from whence come the young and old lion, the viper and fiery flying serpent, they will carry their riches upon the shoulders of young asses, and their treasures upon the bunches of camels, to a people that shall not profit them.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:7} % *ISAIAH-30-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-7}

			For the Egyptians shall help in vain, and to no purpose: therefore have I cried concerning this, Their strength is to sit still.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:8} % *ISAIAH-30-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-8}

			Now go, write it before them in a table, and note it in a book, that it may be for the time to come for ever and ever:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:9} % *ISAIAH-30-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-9}

			That this is a rebellious people, lying children, children that will not hear the law of the LORD:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:10} % *ISAIAH-30-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-10}

			Which say to the seers, See not; and to the prophets, Prophesy not unto us right things, speak unto us smooth things, prophesy deceits:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:11} % *ISAIAH-30-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-11}

			Get you out of the way, turn aside out of the path, cause the Holy One of Israel to cease from before us.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:12} % *ISAIAH-30-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-12}

			Wherefore thus saith the Holy One of Israel, Because ye despise this word, and trust in oppression and perverseness, and stay thereon:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:13} % *ISAIAH-30-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-13}

			Therefore this iniquity shall be to you as a breach ready to fall, swelling out in a high wall, whose breaking cometh suddenly at an instant.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:14} % *ISAIAH-30-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-14}

			And he shall break it as the breaking of the potters' vessel that is broken in pieces; he shall not spare: so that there shall not be found in the bursting of it a sherd to take fire from the hearth, or to take water withal out of the pit.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]

					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:15} % *ISAIAH-30-15
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-15}

			For thus saith the Lord GOD, the Holy One of Israel; In returning and rest shall ye be saved; in quietness and in confidence shall be your strength: and ye would not.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:16} % *ISAIAH-30-16
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-16}

			But ye said, No; for we will flee upon horses; therefore shall ye flee: and, We will ride upon the swift; therefore shall they that pursue you be swift.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:17} % *ISAIAH-30-17
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-17}

			One thousand shall flee at the rebuke of one; at the rebuke of five shall ye flee: till ye be left as a beacon upon the top of a mountain, and as an ensign on an hill.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:18} % *ISAIAH-30-18
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-18}

			And therefore will the LORD wait, that he may be gracious unto you, and therefore will he be exalted, that he may have mercy upon you: for the LORD is a God of judgment: blessed are all they that wait for him.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:19} % *ISAIAH-30-19
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-19}

			For the people shall dwell in Zion at Jerusalem: thou shalt weep no more: he will be very gracious unto thee at the voice of thy cry; when he shall hear it, he will answer thee.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTR1]

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:20} % *ISAIAH-30-20
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-20}

			And though the Lord give you the bread of adversity, and the water of affliction, yet shall not thy teachers be removed into a corner any more, but thine eyes shall see thy teachers:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTR1]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:21} % *ISAIAH-30-21
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-21}

			And thine ears shall hear a word behind thee, saying, This is the way, walk ye in it, when ye turn to the right hand, and when ye turn to the left.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-22}

			Ye shall defile also the covering of thy graven images of silver, and the ornament of thy molten images of gold: thou shalt cast them away as a menstruous cloth; thou shalt say unto it, Get thee hence.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-23}

			Then shall he give the rain of thy seed, that thou shalt sow the ground withal; and bread of the increase of the earth, and it shall be fat and plenteous: in that day shall thy cattle feed in large pastures.

		% % ---

			The oxen likewise and the young asses that ear the ground shall eat clean provender, which hath been winnowed with the shovel and with the fan.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-25}

			And there shall be upon every high mountain, and upon every high hill, rivers and streams of waters in the day of the great slaughter, when the towers fall.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-26}

			Moreover the light of the moon shall be as the light of the sun, and the light of the sun shall be sevenfold, as the light of seven days, in the day that the LORD bindeth up the breach of his people, and healeth the stroke of their wound.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-27}

			Behold, the name of the LORD cometh from far, burning with his anger, and the burden thereof is heavy: his lips are full of indignation, and his tongue as a devouring fire:

		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:28} % *ISAIAH-30-28
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-28}

			And his breath, as an overflowing stream, shall reach to the midst of the neck, to sift the nations with the sieve of vanity: and there shall be a bridle in the jaws of the people, causing them to err.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:29} % *ISAIAH-30-29
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-29}

			Ye shall have a song, as in the night when a holy solemnity is kept; and gladness of heart, as when one goeth with a pipe to come into the mountain of the LORD, to the mighty One of Israel.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:30} % *ISAIAH-30-30
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-30}

			And the LORD shall cause his glorious voice to be heard, and shall shew the lighting down of his arm, with the indignation of his anger, and with the flame of a devouring fire, with scattering, and tempest, and hailstones.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 30:31} % *ISAIAH-30-31
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-31}

			For through the voice of the LORD shall the Assyrian be beaten down, which smote with a rod.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-32}

			And in every place where the grounded staff shall pass, which the LORD shall lay upon him, it shall be with tabrets and harps: and in battles of shaking will he fight with it.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-30-33}

			For Tophet is ordained of old; yea, for the king it is prepared; he hath made it deep and large: the pile thereof is fire and much wood; the breath of the LORD, like a stream of brimstone, doth kindle it.

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 31} % *ISAIAH-31
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-31}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:1} % *ISAIAH-31-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-1}

			Woe to them that go down to Egypt for help; and stay on horses, and trust in chariots, because they are many; and in horsemen, because they are very strong; but they look not unto the Holy One of Israel, neither seek the LORD!

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:2} % *ISAIAH-31-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-2}

			Yet he also is wise, and will bring evil, and will not call back his words: but will arise against the house of the evildoers, and against the help of them that work iniquity.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:3} % *ISAIAH-31-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-3}

			Now the Egyptians are men, and not God; and their horses flesh, and not spirit. When the LORD shall stretch out his hand, both he that helpeth shall fall, and he that is holpen shall fall down, and they all shall fail together.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:4} % *ISAIAH-31-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-4}

			For thus hath the LORD spoken unto me, Like as the lion and the young lion roaring on his prey, when a multitude of shepherds is called forth against him, he will not be afraid of their voice, nor abase himself for the noise of them: so shall the LORD of hosts come down to fight for mount Zion, and for the hill thereof.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:5} % *ISAIAH-31-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-5}

			As birds flying, so will the LORD of hosts defend Jerusalem; defending also he will deliver it; and passing over he will preserve it.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:6} % *ISAIAH-31-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-6}

			Turn ye unto him from whom the children of Israel have deeply revolted.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:7} % *ISAIAH-31-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-7}

			For in that day every man shall cast away his idols of silver, and his idols of gold, which your own hands have made unto you for a sin.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:8} % *ISAIAH-31-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-8}

			Then shall the Assyrian fall with the sword, not of a mighty man; and the sword, not of a mean man, shall devour him: but he shall flee from the sword, and his young men shall be discomfited.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 31:9} % *ISAIAH-31-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-31-9}

			And he shall pass over to his strong hold for fear, and his princes shall be afraid of the ensign, saith the LORD, whose fire is in Zion, and his furnace in Jerusalem.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 32:2} % *ISAIAH-32-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-32-2}

			And a man shall be as an hiding place from the wind, and a covert from the tempest; as rivers of water in a dry place, as the shadow of a great rock in a weary land.

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 32:3} % *ISAIAH-32-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-32-3}

			And the eyes of them that see shall not be dim, and the ears of them that hear shall hearken.

			% \begin{framed}

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-32-6}

			For the vile person will speak villany, and his heart will work iniquity, to practise hypocrisy, and to utter error against the LORD, to make empty the soul of the hungry, and he will cause the drink of the thirsty to fail.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-32-7}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 33} % *ISAIAH-33
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-33}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:1} % *ISAIAH-33-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-1}

			Woe to thee that spoilest, and thou wast not spoiled; and dealest treacherously, and they dealt not treacherously with thee! when thou shalt cease to spoil, thou shalt be spoiled; and when thou shalt make an end to deal treacherously, they shall deal treacherously with thee.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]

					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:2} % *ISAIAH-33-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-2}

			O LORD, be gracious unto us; we have waited for thee: be thou their arm every morning, our salvation also in the time of trouble.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:3} % *ISAIAH-33-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-3}

			At the noise of the tumult the people fled; at the lifting up of thyself the nations were scattered.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:4} % *ISAIAH-33-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-4}

			And your spoil shall be gathered like the gathering of the caterpiller: as the running to and fro of locusts shall he run upon them.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:5} % *ISAIAH-33-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-5}

			The LORD is exalted; for he dwelleth on high: he hath filled Zion with judgment and righteousness.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:6} % *ISAIAH-33-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-6}

			And wisdom and knowledge shall be the stability of thy times, and strength of salvation: the fear of the LORD is his treasure.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:7} % *ISAIAH-33-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-7}

			Behold, their valiant ones shall cry without: the ambassadors of peace shall weep bitterly.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:8} % *ISAIAH-33-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-8}

			The highways lie waste, the wayfaring man ceaseth: he hath broken the covenant, he hath despised the cities, he regardeth no man.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 33:9} % *ISAIAH-33-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-33-9}

			The earth mourneth and languisheth: Lebanon is ashamed and hewn down: Sharon is like a wilderness; and Bashan and Carmel shake off their fruits.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-34-1}

		% % ---

			And all the host of heaven shall be dissolved, and the heavens shall be rolled together as a scroll: and all their host shall fall down, as the leaf falleth off from the vine, and as a falling fig from the fig tree.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 34:5} % *ISAIAH-34-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-34-5}

			For my sword shall be bathed in heaven: behold, it shall come down upon Idumea, and upon the people of my curse, to judgment.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[BHS]  ``shall be bathed'' = \he{riw:*tAh}, a form of \he{rAwAh}
					\item[LXX] \gr{ἐμεθύσθη ἡ μάχαιρά μου ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ· ἰδοὺ ἐπὶ τὴν Ιδουμαίαν καταβήσεται καὶ ἐπὶ τὸν λαὸν τῆς ἀπωλείας μετὰ κρίσεως.}
					% \item[BSV]
					\item[ASV] For my sword hath drunk its fill in heaven: behold, it shall come down upon Edom, and upon the people of my curse, to judgment.
					\item[NASB] For My sword is satiated in heaven, Behold it shall descend for judgment upon Edom And upon the people whom I have devoted to destruction.
					\item[NIV] My sword has drunk its fill in the heavens; see, it descends in judgment on Edom, the people I have totally destroyed.
					\item[NRSV] When my sword has drunk its fill in the heavens, lo, it will descend upon Edom, upon the people I have doomed to judgment.
					\item[NICOT] When my sword is drunken in the heavens, behold, upon Edom it shall fall, upon my devoted people, for judgment.
						\begin{compactenum}
							\item Here NICOT (page 609) has the following footnote: ``1QIs\supers{a} has \textit{tr'h}, `will be seen,' instead of \textit{rwth}, `drunken.' Driver (\textit{JSS} 13 [1968] 55) thinks that MT is incredible. So it is if taken too literally, but cf. \hyperref[DEUTERONOMY-32-41]{Deuteronomy 32:41--43} for a similar figure. \textit{BHS} emends `my sword' to `the sword of the Lord' and `my devoted people' to `his devoted people.' But there is no real ms. support for these changes.'' The ``will be seen'' interpretation would make more natural sense, both those verses in Deuteronomy do have similar language. Will be interesting to see if Quinta makes any changes here. Also, why is it that I find so many connections to Deuteronomy 32 in restoration scripture?
						\end{compactenum}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[JUSTNT*]
					% \item[OTX*]
					% \item[OTXX*]
					% \item[OTXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXXXX*]
					% \item[NETS]
					% \item[OTR1]
					% \item[OTR2]
					% \item[OTX*]
					% \item[OTXX*]
					% \item[OTXXX*]
					% \item[OTXXXX*]

					% \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

			And the streams thereof shall be turned into pitch, and the dust thereof into brimstone, and the land thereof shall become burning pitch.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-34-10}

			It shall not be quenched night nor day; the smoke thereof shall go up for ever: from generation to generation it shall lie waste; none shall pass through it for ever and ever.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-34-11}

			But the cormorant and the bittern shall possess it; the owl also and the raven shall dwell in it: and he shall stretch out upon it the line of confusion, and the stones of emptiness.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-34-12}

			They shall call the nobles thereof to the kingdom, but none shall be there, and all her princes shall be nothing.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-34-13}

			And thorns shall come up in her palaces, nettles and brambles in the fortresses thereof: and it shall be an habitation of dragons, and a court for owls.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 35:1} % *ISAIAH-35-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-1}

			The wilderness and the solitary place shall be glad for them; and the desert shall rejoice, and blossom as the rose.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 35:2} % *ISAIAH-35-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-2}

			It shall blossom abundantly, and rejoice even with joy and singing: the glory of Lebanon shall be given unto it, the excellency of Carmel and Sharon, they shall see the glory of the LORD, and the excellency of our God.

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 35:3} % *ISAIAH-35-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-3}

			Strengthen ye the weak hands, and confirm the feeble knees.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-4}

			Say to them that are of a fearful heart, Be strong, fear not: behold, your God will come with vengeance, even God with a recompence; he will come and save you.

				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-5}

			Then the eyes of the blind shall be opened, and the ears of the deaf shall be unstopped.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-6}

			Then shall the lame man leap as an hart, and the tongue of the dumb sing: for in the wilderness shall waters break out, and streams in the desert.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 35:7} % *ISAIAH-35-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-7}

			And the parched ground shall become a pool, and the thirsty land springs of water: in the habitation of dragons, where each lay, shall be grass with reeds and rushes.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 35:8} % *ISAIAH-35-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-8}

			And an highway shall be there, and a way, and it shall be called The way of holiness; the unclean shall not pass over it; but it shall be for those: the wayfaring men, though fools, shall not err therein.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 35:9} % *ISAIAH-35-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-9}

			No lion shall be there, nor any ravenous beast shall go up thereon, it shall not be found there; but the redeemed shall walk there:

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 35:10} % *ISAIAH-35-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-35-10}

			And the ransomed of the LORD shall return, and come to Zion with songs and everlasting joy upon their heads: they shall obtain joy and gladness, and sorrow and sighing shall flee away.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 36} % *ISAIAH-36
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-36}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:1} % *ISAIAH-36-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-1}

			Now it came to pass in the fourteenth year of king Hezekiah, that Sennacherib king of Assyria came up against all the defenced cities of Judah, and took them.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:2} % *ISAIAH-36-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-2}

			And the king of Assyria sent Rabshakeh from Lachish to Jerusalem unto king Hezekiah with a great army. And he stood by the conduit of the upper pool in the highway of the fuller's field.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:3} % *ISAIAH-36-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-3}

			Then came forth unto him Eliakim, Hilkiah's son, which was over the house, and Shebna the scribe, and Joah, Asaph's son, the recorder.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:4} % *ISAIAH-36-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-4}

			And Rabshakeh said unto them, Say ye now to Hezekiah, Thus saith the great king, the king of Assyria, What confidence is this wherein thou trustest?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:5} % *ISAIAH-36-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-5}

			I say, sayest thou, (but they are but vain words) I have counsel and strength for war: now on whom dost thou trust, that thou rebellest against me?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:6} % *ISAIAH-36-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-6}

			Lo, thou trustest in the staff of this broken reed, on Egypt; whereon if a man lean, it will go into his hand, and pierce it: so is Pharaoh king of Egypt to all that trust in him.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:7} % *ISAIAH-36-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-7}

			But if thou say to me, We trust in the LORD our God: is it not he, whose high places and whose altars Hezekiah hath taken away, and said to Judah and to Jerusalem, Ye shall worship before this altar?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:8} % *ISAIAH-36-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-8}

			Now therefore give pledges, I pray thee, to my master the king of Assyria, and I will give thee two thousand horses, if thou be able on thy part to set riders upon them.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:9} % *ISAIAH-36-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-9}

			How then wilt thou turn away the face of one captain of the least of my master's servants, and put thy trust on Egypt for chariots and for horsemen?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:10} % *ISAIAH-36-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-10}

			And am I now come up without the LORD against this land to destroy it? the LORD said unto me, Go up against this land, and destroy it.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:11} % *ISAIAH-36-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-11}

			Then said Eliakim and Shebna and Joah unto Rabshakeh, Speak, I pray thee, unto thy servants in the Syrian language; for we understand it: and speak not to us in the Jews' language, in the ears of the people that are on the wall.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:12} % *ISAIAH-36-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-12}

			But Rabshakeh said, Hath my master sent me to thy master and to thee to speak these words? hath he not sent me to the men that sit upon the wall, that they may eat their own dung, and drink their own piss with you?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:13} % *ISAIAH-36-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-13}

			Then Rabshakeh stood, and cried with a loud voice in the Jews' language, and said, Hear ye the words of the great king, the king of Assyria.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:14} % *ISAIAH-36-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-14}

			Thus saith the king, Let not Hezekiah deceive you: for he shall not be able to deliver you.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:15} % *ISAIAH-36-15
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-15}

			Neither let Hezekiah make you trust in the LORD, saying, The LORD will surely deliver us: this city shall not be delivered into the hand of the king of Assyria.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:16} % *ISAIAH-36-16
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-16}

			Hearken not to Hezekiah: for thus saith the king of Assyria, Make an agreement with me by a present, and come out to me: and eat ye every one of his vine, and every one of his fig tree, and drink ye every one the waters of his own cistern;

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:17} % *ISAIAH-36-17
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-17}

			Until I come and take you away to a land like your own land, a land of corn and wine, a land of bread and vineyards.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:18} % *ISAIAH-36-18
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-18}

			Beware lest Hezekiah persuade you, saying, The LORD will deliver us. Hath any of the gods of the nations delivered his land out of the hand of the king of Assyria?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:19} % *ISAIAH-36-19
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-19}

			Where are the gods of Hamath and Arphad? where are the gods of Sepharvaim? and have they delivered Samaria out of my hand?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:20} % *ISAIAH-36-20
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-20}

			Who are they among all the gods of these lands, that have delivered their land out of my hand, that the LORD should deliver Jerusalem out of my hand?

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:21} % *ISAIAH-36-21
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-21}

			But they held their peace, and answered him not a word: for the king's commandment was, saying, Answer him not.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 36:22} % *ISAIAH-36-22
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-36-22}

			Then came Eliakim, the son of Hilkiah, that was over the household, and Shebna the scribe, and Joah, the son of Asaph, the recorder, to Hezekiah with their clothes rent, and told him the words of Rabshakeh.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}
	% -----
	\subsubsection{Isaiah 37} % *ISAIAH-37
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-37}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:1} % *ISAIAH-37-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-37-1}

			And it came to pass, when king Hezekiah heard it, that he rent his clothes, and covered himself with sackcloth, and went into the house of the LORD.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:2} % *ISAIAH-37-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-37-2}

			And he sent Eliakim, who was over the household, and Shebna the scribe, and the elders of the priests covered with sackcloth, unto Isaiah the prophet the son of Amoz.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:3} % *ISAIAH-37-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-37-3}

			And they said unto him, Thus saith Hezekiah, This day is a day of trouble, and of rebuke, and of blasphemy: for the children are come to the birth, and there is not strength to bring forth.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:4} % *ISAIAH-37-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-37-4}

			It may be the LORD thy God will hear the words of Rabshakeh, whom the king of Assyria his master hath sent to reproach the living God, and will reprove the words which the LORD thy God hath heard: wherefore lift up thy prayer for the remnant that is left.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:5} % *ISAIAH-37-5
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-37-5}

			So the servants of king Hezekiah came to Isaiah.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:6} % *ISAIAH-37-6
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-37-6}

			And Isaiah said unto them, Thus shall ye say unto your master, Thus saith the LORD, Be not afraid of the words that thou hast heard, wherewith the servants of the king of Assyria have blasphemed me.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:7} % *ISAIAH-37-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-37-7}

			Behold, I will send a blast upon him, and he shall hear a rumour, and return to his own land; and I will cause him to fall by the sword in his own land.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]
					% % \item[AB ]
					% % \item[NETS]
					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]
					% % \item[OTX*]
					% % \item[OTXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:8} % *ISAIAH-37-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-37-8}

			So Rabshakeh returned, and found the king of Assyria warring against Libnah: for he had heard that he was departed from Lachish.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}
					% % \item[BSV]
					% % \item[ASV]

					% % \item[OTR1]
					% % \item[OTR2]

					% % \item[OTXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXX*]
					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 37:9} % *ISAIAH-37-9
		% % ---

		% % ---

		% % ---

		% % ---

			And it came to pass, as he was worshipping in the house of Nisroch his god, that Adrammelech and Sharezer his sons smote him with the sword; and they escaped into the land of Armenia: and Esarhaddon his son reigned in his stead.

			In those days was Hezekiah sick unto death. And Isaiah the prophet the son of Amoz came unto him, and said unto him, Thus saith the LORD, Set thine house in order: for thou shalt die, and not live.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-5}

			Go, and say to Hezekiah, Thus saith the LORD, the God of David thy father, I have heard thy prayer, I have seen thy tears: behold, I will add unto thy days fifteen years.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-6}

			And I will deliver thee and this city out of the hand of the king of Assyria: and I will defend this city.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-7}

			And this shall be a sign unto thee from the LORD, that the LORD will do this thing that he hath spoken;

		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:8} % *ISAIAH-38-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-8}

			Behold, I will bring again the shadow of the degrees, which is gone down in the sun dial of Ahaz, ten degrees backward. So the sun returned ten degrees, by which degrees it was gone down.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:9} % *ISAIAH-38-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-9}

			The writing of Hezekiah king of Judah, when he had been sick, and was recovered of his sickness:

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:10} % *ISAIAH-38-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-10}

			I said in the cutting off of my days, I shall go to the gates of the grave: I am deprived of the residue of my years.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:11} % *ISAIAH-38-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-11}

			I said, I shall not see the LORD, even the LORD, in the land of the living: I shall behold man no more with the inhabitants of the world.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:12} % *ISAIAH-38-12
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-12}

			Mine age is departed, and is removed from me as a shepherd's tent: I have cut off like a weaver my life: he will cut me off with pining sickness: from day even to night wilt thou make an end of me.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

					% % \item[OTXXXXXX*]
				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:13} % *ISAIAH-38-13
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-13}

			I reckoned till morning, that, as a lion, so will he break all my bones: from day even to night wilt thou make an end of me.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:14} % *ISAIAH-38-14
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-14}

			Like a crane or a swallow, so did I chatter: I did mourn as a dove: mine eyes fail with looking upward: O LORD, I am oppressed; undertake for me.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:15} % *ISAIAH-38-15
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-15}

			What shall I say? he hath both spoken unto me, and himself hath done it: I shall go softly all my years in the bitterness of my soul.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:16} % *ISAIAH-38-16
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-16}

			O LORD, by these things men live, and in all these things is the life of my spirit: so wilt thou recover me, and make me to live.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:17} % *ISAIAH-38-17
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-17}

			Behold, for peace I had great bitterness: but thou hast in love to my soul delivered it from the pit of corruption: for thou hast cast all my sins behind thy back.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:18} % *ISAIAH-38-18
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-18}

			For the grave cannot praise thee, death can not celebrate thee: they that go down into the pit cannot hope for thy truth.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:19} % *ISAIAH-38-19
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-19}

			The living, the living, he shall praise thee, as I do this day: the father to the children shall make known thy truth.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:20} % *ISAIAH-38-20
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-20}

			The LORD was ready to save me: therefore we will sing my songs to the stringed instruments all the days of our life in the house of the LORD.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 38:21} % *ISAIAH-38-21
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-21}

			For Isaiah had said, Let them take a lump of figs, and lay it for a plaister upon the boil, and he shall recover.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-38-22}

			Hezekiah also had said, What is the sign that I shall go up to the house of the LORD?

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 39} % *ISAIAH-39
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-39}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 39:1} % *ISAIAH-39-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-39-1}

			At that time Merodachbaladan, the son of Baladan, king of Babylon, sent letters and a present to Hezekiah: for he had heard that he had been sick, and was recovered.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
					% % \item[LXX] \gr{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 39:2} % *ISAIAH-39-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-39-2}

			And Hezekiah was glad of them, and shewed them the house of his precious things, the silver, and the gold, and the spices, and the precious ointment, and all the house of his armour, and all that was found in his treasures: there was nothing in his house, nor in all his dominion, that Hezekiah shewed them not.

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 39:3} % *ISAIAH-39-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-39-3}

			Then came Isaiah the prophet unto king Hezekiah, and said unto him, What said these men? and from whence came they unto thee? And Hezekiah said, They are come from a far country unto me, even from Babylon.

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 39:4} % *ISAIAH-39-4
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-39-4}

			Then said he, What have they seen in thine house? And Hezekiah answered, All that is in mine house have they seen: there is nothing among my treasures that I have not shewed them.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-39-5}

			Then said Isaiah to Hezekiah, Hear the word of the LORD of hosts:

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-39-6}

			Behold, the days come, that all that is in thine house, and that which thy fathers have laid up in store until this day, shall be carried to Babylon: nothing shall be left, saith the LORD.

		% % ---

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-39-7}

			And of thy sons that shall issue from thee, which thou shalt beget, shall they take away; and they shall be eunuchs in the palace of the king of Babylon.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-39-8}

			Then said Hezekiah to Isaiah, Good is the word of the LORD which thou hast spoken. He said moreover, For there shall be peace and truth in my days.

	\subsubsection{Isaiah 40} % *ISAIAH-40
	% -----
		\label{ISAIAH-40}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 40:1} % *ISAIAH-40-1
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-1}

			Comfort ye, comfort ye my people, saith your God.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 40:2} % *ISAIAH-40-2
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-2}

			Speak ye comfortably to Jerusalem, and cry unto her, that her warfare is accomplished, that her iniquity is pardoned: for she hath received of the LORD's hand double for all her sins.

			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}

		\paragraph{Isaiah 40:3} % *ISAIAH-40-3
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-3}

			The voice of him that crieth in the wilderness, Prepare ye the way of the LORD, make straight in the desert a highway for our God.

				\begin{compactdesc}

			And the glory of the LORD shall be revealed, and all flesh shall see it together: for the mouth of the LORD hath spoken it.

		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-6}

			The voice said, Cry. And he said, What shall I cry? All flesh is grass, and all the goodliness thereof is as the flower of the field:

			% \scriptsize

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 40:7} % *ISAIAH-40-7
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-7}

			The grass withereth, the flower fadeth: because the spirit of the LORD bloweth upon it: surely the people is grass.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 40:8} % *ISAIAH-40-8
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-8}

			The grass withereth, the flower fadeth: but the word of our God shall stand for ever.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 40:9} % *ISAIAH-40-9
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-9}

			O Zion, that bringest good tidings, get thee up into the high mountain; O Jerusalem, that bringest good tidings, lift up thy voice with strength; lift it up, be not afraid; say unto the cities of Judah, Behold your God!

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

				% \end{compactdesc}
			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 40:10} % *ISAIAH-40-10
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-10}

			Behold, the Lord GOD will come with strong hand, and his arm shall rule for him: behold, his reward is with him, and his work before him.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}

			% \end{framed}

		% % ---
		\paragraph{Isaiah 40:11} % *ISAIAH-40-11
		% % ---
			\label{ISAIAH-40-11}

			He shall feed his flock like a shepherd: he shall gather the lambs with his arm, and carry them in his bosom, and shall gently lead those that are with young.

			% \begin{framed}
			% \scriptsize
				% \begin{compactdesc}
					% % \item[BHS] \he{}
